Mehrfaches Ausführen des Wächter-Skripts im selben Fenster ändert nichts mehr.
- `rescan_library` verlangt über MCP die Bestätigung am Fernseher. Die Liste der angemeldeten Sitzungen ist fail-closed (eine fehlgeschlagene Abfrage macht sie „unbekannt“), und Windows-Update bekommt nie den Couch- oder Entsperr-Grant.

**Sicherheit: Dienste pausieren und Updates brauchen mehr als ein beliebiges Konto (Audit L-1, L-2)**
- Dienste pausieren (`PauseServices`) geht ohne Freigabe nur aus der Konsolensitzung oder als erhöhter Administrator, aus jeder anderen Sitzung nur mit der Freigabe von Eltern oder Administrator. Ist die Konsolensitzung nicht zu lesen, braucht auch die Konsole eine Freigabe.
- Rückkehr in den Couch-Modus aus einer Nicht-Konsolensitzung (RDP, zweite Sitzung): Dienste pausieren braucht dort eine Freigabe, also zeigt jede Rückkehr eine UAC-Administratorabfrage. Sie kann bis zu etwa 65 s warten, und ein folgendes Fortsetzen der Dienste wartet dahinter. Auf der Konsole bleibt alles wie bisher.
- Windows-Update-Installation und CouchHUD-Update brauchen dieselbe Freigabe, sobald ein anderes Konto angemeldet ist oder die Sitzungen nicht zu lesen sind. CouchHUD fragt die UAC-Bestätigung dann an und startet danach neu; eine von der Prüfung schon verbrauchte Freigabe erhält die Sitzung des Aufrufers beim Update.

**Sicherheit: MCP ohne Bestätigungs-Schritt (Audit L-3)**
- Ein änderndes Werkzeug über MCP wird nicht mehr ohne Rückfrage ausgeführt, wenn keine Bestätigung am Fernseher verfügbar ist (fail-closed). Im HUD ist sie immer gesetzt.

**Behoben: Tray-Symbol des Desktop-Modus**
- Das Symbol im Infobereich, das im Desktop-Modus (PC-Modus) erscheint, blieb nach der Rückkehr zur Couch bestehen, und sein Eintrag *Beenden* beendete CouchHUD ohne Code, auch bei gesperrter Couch. Das Symbol verschwindet jetzt mit dem Verlassen des Desktop-Modus, und *Beenden* gilt nur im Desktop-Modus.

**Tab-Leiste: Skin-Prüfungen von 0.6.4 (die Laufzeit-Festhaltungen sind in der strukturellen Leiste aufgegangen)**
- Die Laufzeit-Festhaltungen an Tabs und Leiste, der Treffertest, der Rückfall auf den Standard-Skin und die Skin-Prüfung für verdeckte, zerdrückte oder unlesbare Tabs aus 0.6.4 gibt es nicht mehr: Kein Selektor eines Skins erreicht die Leiste, und nichts kann darüber malen (siehe „Tab-Leiste: strukturell gesichert statt zur Laufzeit geprüft“ weiter oben).
- Was davon bleibt: Die Skin-Prüfung (`scripts/skin-check.mjs`) meldet weiter `@font-face` mit `size-adjust` unter 80 % oder `ascent`-/`descent`-/`line-gap-override` (das trifft den Statusbereich und jeden anderen Text) sowie Regeln an `html`/`body`, die die ganze Seite ausblenden oder abschneiden. `scripts/tabs-check.mjs` nimmt `--port` und `--debug`, damit zwei Arbeitskopien gleichzeitig prüfen können, und nimmt den Katalogordner auch aus `COUCHHUD_CATALOG_SKINS`; es prüft zusätzlich die absichtlich feindlichen Test-Skins unter `scripts/fixtures/skins`: Keiner darf einen Rückfall brauchen, jeder Tab muss sichtbar und lesbar sein. Das Restrisiko ist in [docs/SKINS.md](docs/SKINS.md) genannt.
**Katalog-Skins passen zur neuen Tab-Leiste** (neue Pakete im Katalog: Grünlicht 1.0.3, Neonstadt 1.0.2, Nova 1.0.2, Synthwave 1.0.2)
- Grünlicht und Nova: schmaleres Polster in den Tab-Pillen (0,8 und 0,95 rem statt 1,3 und 1,4 rem), damit alle Tabs mit dem üblichen Statusbereich in voller Breite passen, statt zu scrollen.
- Neonstadt: schmaleres Polster (0,5 statt 0,75 rem), damit die Leiste nicht mehr schrumpft und der Name im Fokus („Smart Home“) nicht mehr angeschnitten durchläuft; die abgeschnittene Ecke des aktiven Tabs ist zurück, jetzt mit dem Hintergrund gezeichnet statt mit `clip-path`, das CouchHUD an Tabs nicht zulässt.
- Synthwave: der Leuchtschein um den aktiven Tab endete an dessen Rand als dunkler Kasten; der Tab hat jetzt Platz dafür (Polster, vom Abstand abgezogen), der Strich darunter ist so breit wie der Name, der Schein etwas enger. Die Tabs sind etwas kleiner und rücken enger zusammen, damit sie passen.

**VM-Test 0.7 (Jugendschutz): Nachbesserungen**
- Profile anlegen und löschen fragen auf der gesperrten Couch jetzt zuerst nach dem Couch-Code und danach nach dem Eltern-Code (beim Anlegen noch vor Name, Symbol und Farbe). Vorher kam erst der Eltern-Code, dann UAC, und der Host lehnte mit „Gesperrt: erst entsperren“ ab. Dieselbe Reihenfolge gilt jetzt für alle Änderungen an Profilen.
- Dienste pausieren: Ein pausierter Dienst bleibt aus. Der Windows-Dienst prüft alle 30 Sekunden alle gehaltenen Dienste (nur die feste Liste, nur solange die Pause gilt) und hält einen wieder gestarteten (verzögerter Autostart wie Windows Search, Trigger-Start wie PcaSvc) erneut an; Starttypen ändert er nie, das Fortsetzen startet ihn wie bisher wieder. Das Protokoll nennt je Dienst das echte Ergebnis (gestoppt, lief beim Pausieren nicht, nicht zu stoppen) statt nur „ok“; die Shell schreibt dazu den Zustand jedes Dienstes.
- Jugendschutz: Handy-Verbindung, MCP-Server und MCP im Heimnetz einzuschalten braucht jetzt mit Jugendschutz den Eltern-Code (nicht mehr nur den Couch-Code); Ausschalten bleibt frei. Die Oberfläche fragt vorher (erst Entsperren, dann Eltern-Code), der Host prüft dasselbe.
- Die Handy-Seite in den Einstellungen meldet eine gesperrte Couch beim Lesen der zweiten Bestätigung nicht mehr als Fehler (kein Hinweis, keine Protokollzeile).
- Seite „Gesperrte Spiele“: ein Hinweis, dass die Sperren zum aktiven Profil gehören.
- Dienste pausieren, Nachbesserung: Die Überwachung gilt nur für lebende Pausen. Hat kein Halter mehr eine Sitzung oder läuft keine CouchHud.exe aus dem Installationsordner mehr, endet die Pause wie ein Fortsetzen, und ein von Hand gestarteter Dienst bleibt an. Ein Fehler beim Schreiben des Zustands, ein fehlgeschlagener Durchlauf und ein fremder Abbruch stehen einmal im Protokoll; die Schleife läuft weiter.
- Netzwerk-Schalter, Nachbesserung: Ist der Eltern-Grant nach dem Start des Zugangs weg (zwischendurch verbraucht), wird die Änderung zurückgenommen und der Zugang wieder geschlossen. „MCP im Heimnetz“ nimmt sich bei einem Fehler selbst zurück wie die beiden anderen. Pause: Schlägt der Start nach dem Ende einer Pause fehl, bleibt sie bestehen und der Start wird wiederholt, ohne erneutes Anhalten.

### English

**Tab bar: secured by structure instead of checked at runtime (the skin API of the bar)**
- The tab bar now lives in a **closed shadow DOM** and as a **popover in the top layer** (`popover="manual"`, `showPopover()`). A skin's selectors no longer reach a tab (`#tab-list li`, `#tabs`, `.tab-more`, `.glyph.shoulder` and `::first-letter`/`::first-line` on the tabs do nothing), and no layer of the page can paint over it, not even a `body::after` with `z-index: 9999`. So the bypasses found in the 0.6.4 reviews are no longer detected but impossible: slits in layers on the sampled rows, `@font-face` with `size-adjust` plus `ascent-`/`descent-override`, `::first-letter`, many `text-shadow`, layers of `body`/`html` over the bar.
- The tab area starts with `all: initial` (font, colour, shadows, outline, `font-size-adjust` and spacing of `body`/`html` do not inherit into it); the host `#top` is pinned from inside with `!important` (which also beats a page's `!important`), its `::before`/`::after`/`::backdrop` are off; the status area stays in the page, is slotted into the bar and limited to a box of its own. On `html`/`body` the program only holds `zoom`, `content-visibility` and `display`.
- **New skin API:** a small list of custom properties (`--tab-color`, `--tab-color-active`, `--tab-accent`, `--tab-bg`, `--tab-bg-active`, `--tab-glow`, `--tab-glow-color`, `--tab-font`, `--tab-size`, `--tab-weight`, `--tab-weight-active`, `--tab-letter`, `--tab-case`, `--tab-gap`, `--tab-pad-x`, `--tab-pad-y`, `--tab-radius`, `--tab-cut`, `--tab-underline`, `--tab-min-width`, `--bar-bg`, `--bar-border`, `--bar-radius`, `--bar-pad`, `--bar-glass`, `--bar-pad-top`, `--tab-glyph-*`). Every value is cleaned and clamped in JavaScript (core/tab-skin.js): colours stay readable (`readableColor`, against the tab's fill, the pill and `--bg`), glow and blur are capped (at most one shadow, 1.2 rem), sizes, gaps, weight and letter spacing lie in fixed ranges, fonts come only from a list of system fonts and generic families, never from a skin's `@font-face` (a name a skin declares itself drops out of the tabs' list).
- **What stands over the bar:** only what covers the whole screen or never needs room (boot screen, screensaver, launch screen, the web page's full screen mark and the mouse pointer, the last two as shadow roots of their own); they are popovers themselves and are opened only while the program has them open, after the bar and in the old order of their `z-index` (core/layers.js). The Guide menu, dialogs, toasts and notifications stand under the bar (the Guide menu starts below it, the bar dims): so no rule of a skin for the size, children, pseudo elements or 3D of these parts ever reaches a tab.
- **Removed**, because the structure makes it unnecessary: the inline holds on tabs, arrows and bar, the hit test with its layer scoring, the measurement of the text height, the guard for the program's overlays, the hold on the page's ancestors (except `zoom`, `content-visibility`, `display`) and the skin lint for hidden, squashed or covered tabs. A minimal check is left: the bar is in the top layer, has a size and every tab has room; otherwise it is shown again once and the default skin is used if need be. `skin-check.mjs` still reports errors for `@font-face` tricks and for `html`/`body` rules that hide the page, and as notes rules for the old bar and `--tab-*` values that are not a plain number.
- Follow-ups after the independent review: the mouse pointer is a closed shadow root of its own (`all: initial`, `::backdrop` off, a box of at most 8 rem) and stands in the top layer only while the mouse is in use. The bar's host starts with `all: initial !important` (anchor positioning, `aspect-ratio` and container queries are covered); the bar measures in pixels from the program's rem instead of the document's, and `html` keeps its font size inline. Behind the bar there is always `--bg` at 80 % opacity; the readability of the text colours holds over black and over white behind it. The status area is limited to 40 % of the width and a fixed height. The minimal check also looks whether the bar is at the top of the screen and tabs have a readable height and font. Fonts and values are read again when a media query changes. The server removes every `@import` and `@layer` statement from a skin's CSS (the content of an `@layer` block stays as ordinary rules), and the program relies on cascade layers nowhere any more. `tabs-check` checks every fixture skin (now over 110, the reviewer's included) against the pixels behind the letters and opens the Guide menu and a dialog for each.
- The sanitizer for skin CSS reads by the CSS syntax (an unquoted `url(`, strings that end at a newline, a carriage return or a form feed, escapes) and, as a backstop, refuses the whole stylesheet with a warning in the log when `@layer` or `@import` is still anywhere after that; C# and the preview server share one list of cases (`tests/skin-css-cases.json`), and `tabs-check` has Edge parse 2800 sanitized stylesheets (fixed-seed fuzz corpus) with no layer or import rule left. A mouse click on a tab no longer leaves the code prompt, the time-up page or a required profile picker (as LB/RB do not).
- Skin CSS is always read as UTF-8 (header `charset=utf-8` for CSS and SVG, which beats an `@charset`): an `@charset` is removed, and the skin is refused whole (warning in the log) if an `@charset` names anything but `utf-8`, the file is not valid UTF-8 or starts with a UTF-16 mark, or a control character other than tab, line feed, form feed and carriage return is in it. Reason: in `iso-2022-jp` ESC `(B` produces nothing, so `@\x1b(Blayer` and `ht\x1b(Btps://` looked like plain text to the checks and like `@layer` and a web address to the browser. `tabs-check` shows in Edge that the attack works without the header and not with it.
- Backward compatible: old skins still load; their rules for the bar do nothing, and the bar then looks like the default skin's with the skin's colours (`--fg`, `--muted`, `--accent`, `--font`). How a skin moves over is in [docs/SKINS.en.md](docs/SKINS.en.md) (section "The tab bar", with an old/new table and the residual risk). The interface needs WebView2's popovers (runtime 114 and later; the setup brings a current one).
- Tests: the skin API (ranges, readability, fonts, no text getting through), the program's layers, source pins; `tabs-check.mjs` reads the bar through a test hook that only the preview with the mock API sets (never the app) and covers all catalog skins, the hostile skins (new: `hostile-host`, `hostile-vars` and the ten skins of the 0.6.4 reviews as `review-*`) with a hit test, the font list and a screenshot in which every tab must really show letters (`scripts/png.mjs`), and the program's layers.

**Plugin SDK 5, additive: service allowed?, CouchHUD's state, stopping, settings**
- New: `IPluginContext.ServiceAllowed(service)` and `ServiceAllowedChanged`: true only while Offline is off, the service's switch in the Privacy tab is on and the plugin is not paused. An unknown or undeclared service is always `false`. Plus the special token `"hosts": ["lan"]` for a target in the home network that the user types in: its own switch in the Privacy tab, counts as a new permission (parents' code, in the confirmation text); `Http` stays closed for the home network.
- New: capability `host.state` and `IPluginContext.Host` (read only): version, shell or app mode, PC mode, running app (only if not hidden and its area is on), end of the sleep timer, the lock and a throttled `Changed`.
- New: `Events.HostStopping` (when quitting, two seconds for all plugins together) and `Settings.SettingChanged` (key of the plugin's own settings). Brands (`samsungtv`, `anthropic`, `openai`) are reserved for third parties, also in the display name; plain words (`streaming`, `fancontrol`, `weather`, `news`) only as the whole name or the first word (`weather-plus` is taken, `my-weather-widget` is free). Plugin entries whose id starts with `plugin:`, `steam:` or `dir:` are skipped. SDK 4 plugins run unchanged.
- **Behaviour change:** `GameStarted` and `GameEnded` no longer reach plugins for hidden entries and entries of switched-off areas; id and name are cleaned, `InstallDir` stays. Child profiles are not filtered separately. `lan` is a declaration with a privacy switch, not a technical fence.
- New: `LibraryItem.LimitGroup` for plugins: entries with the same group share the parents' time limit and lock even when many have the same install folder; the host puts the plugin id in front, so a plugin never meets the key of another plugin or of a core entry. Without it nothing changes.

**Plugin networking hardened**
- The services and hosts a plugin declares are now part of the signed package statement and of the list of approved plugins. An update that adds a service, a host or `*` counts like a new permission (approval again, also for automatic updates); the hosts are shown in the approval text. The `plugin.json` may not declare more than the statement, and a third-party plugin never gets more hosts at load than were approved. Older statements without services stay valid.
- Third-party SDK 5 plugins that declare services may not build a connection of their own (`HttpClient`, handlers, sockets, `ClientWebSocket`, `WebClient`, `Dns`, `Ping`, `SmtpClient`): the install is refused, they must use `Http`. Settings › Privacy shows a note that the switches apply to plugins that use CouchHUD's network client. The lint now also knows `Dns`, `Ping`, `SmtpClient` and `WinHttpHandler` as network.
- Named hosts only over https; the `*` service only on ports 80 and 443. Answers through the plugin client are capped at 8 MB. The public-address check now sees IPv4 inside IPv6 (NAT64, 6to4, Teredo, compatible) as well as the documentation ranges and 240/4; connecting has a 10 s timeout and connections live at most 2 minutes.
- Calls into weather and news plugins that are not async (place, feed list, setting a place, adding or removing a feed) have a timeout and fail soft instead of blocking the interface. The page loads news pictures straight from the web; while Offline is on, or the news services are switched off, they are left out.
- An API key still in `config.json` also moves into the provider plugin's secrets when its settings file already exists (an existing key is never overwritten); an access error while writing no longer stops the plugin.

**Weather, news and the AI providers are plugins now**
- The weather, the News tab and the assistant's providers (Anthropic, OpenAI) moved out of the core into four plugins of the official catalog: "Weather", "News", "Anthropic" and "OpenAI". Display and controls stay where they were: the weather in the header and in the Guide menu, the News tab, the place search and the feed list in the settings, the assistant with provider, model and key under *AI & MCP*. The assistant itself, its tools and the MCP server stay in the core.
- Without the plugin the feature is absent. If you used it before, a one-time hint offers the plugin from the catalog.
- Carried over on the update: the weather place, feeds you added yourself and the API keys go once into the plugins as their first settings (the keys encrypted again for their plugin, never in plain text) and are then deleted from `config.json`; a key only once the move worked.
- News starts without feeds: the former default feeds (WinFuture and Caschys Blog, or The Verge and PC Gamer) are gone and are not carried over. Feeds on the home network no longer load; plugins reach public addresses only.
- The assistant's tools are now called `plugin_weather_get_weather` and `plugin_news_get_news`; over MCP every plugin tool is confirmed. With the News area off, the news tool is gone too.

**Parental controls: "time is up" can no longer be clicked away (a bug that predates 0.7)**
- The time-up page was not sticky: `clearStack()` took it off the screen on every tab switch, on "Home" and on the Guide menu (a mouse click on a tab, a click on the downloads chip, calls from the host or the phone), and since the guard `timeUp` stayed set it never came back; the game underneath was then visible. Now the page is sticky (`clearStack` never closes it), is put back every minute and when the window is in front again if it is missing and the time is up, and neither tab clicks, LB/RB, the chips of the bar, host or phone navigation nor the Guide menu lead away from it. The browser tab taking over the screen no longer wipes it from the stack either and only restores pages that are still live on its way back; if the time is added or the game ends, the page closes itself, also when an event was missed. A tab click with the Guide menu open closes the menu as everywhere. A code prompt is still cancelled by `clearStack` (nothing is granted). Tests in `ui-logic-check`.

**Privacy: a switch for each network service of a plugin**
- *Settings › Privacy* lists every network service of a loaded plugin with a switch of its own (name, plugin, hosts or "any public address"), behind the parent code under parental controls. The former switches Weather, News and Assistant are gone; what was off there is now off for the matching service. Offline still stops everything.
- Uninstalling a plugin deletes its stored secrets (its other settings stay for a reinstall). For Home Assistant that means signing in again after a reinstall.

**Plugin SDK 5**
- New: network services in `plugin.json` (`services`, the allowed hosts per service) and `IPluginContext.Http(service)`, a client of the host that only goes to declared hosts and public addresses, checks redirects itself and never sends credentials on to another host. Plus the slots `RegisterWeather`, `RegisterNews` and `RegisterChatProvider` with the capabilities `weather`, `news` and `assistant.provider` (CouchHUD's own plugins only). The code lint now counts only making an `HttpClient` as network code, not using the host's client. See `docs/PLUGINS.md`.


**Plugins: a sensor board for fans and temperatures**
- New in the SDK (additive, no new SDK): one of CouchHUD's own plugins puts fan speeds and temperatures on the program's sensor board with `PublishSensors` (capability `sensors.publish`, CouchHUD's own plugins only, a third-party developer cannot even declare it), other plugins read them with `ReadSensors` (capability `sensors.read`, read only). The plugins never talk to each other: the program drops values out of range (fans 0 to 20000 RPM, temperatures −40 to 150 °C, NaN), cleans the names like titles (at most 40 characters), keeps at most 32 values, sets the source itself and stops handing out values older than 30 seconds. A paused plugin (Offline mode) writes nothing, its values are removed, and reads nothing. The values of a plugin that is switched off, uninstalled, unloaded or paused are removed and not handed out either; at most 16 fans and 16 temperatures. The board assumes exactly one publisher. In 0.7.0 no plugin publishes values yet: the board is only the groundwork.
- Plugin management names both capabilities in words. The FanControl plugin in 0.7.0 only switches profiles and shows no readings.
- An unloaded TV plugin leaves no connection behind: a running session to the TV that was made through that plugin is closed when it is unloaded without a restart (errors on closing are swallowed, at most 4 seconds, inside the plugin dispose budget of 5 seconds); sessions of other plugins stay untouched. The TV page now has a browser check (no provider, with a provider and a found device, controller navigation, German and English texts). The compiler warning CS9124 in `CompanionPath.cs` is fixed.

**Plugins: pipes count as network code, `fancontrol` is reserved**
- The code check counts named and anonymous pipes (`System.IO.Pipes`) as network code, because a pipe name can also name another PC (over SMB). A plugin that talks to a program on this PC through a pipe must declare `network:loopback`; without any network capability it is refused.
- The id `fancontrol` belongs to CouchHUD's own plugins (for the new FanControl plugin); others and look-alikes of the id are refused.

**TV control as plugins (SDK 5), Samsung joins**
- The core no longer speaks a TV protocol itself. It keeps *Settings › TV*, the pairing dialog, "turn the TV on with CouchHUD" and "off with the PC", Wake-on-LAN, discovery on the home network, the address rule (home network only, only the paired device) and the key (per TV, DPAPI-encrypted, never logged). The protocol comes from a plugin: **LG webOS** (`webostv`, the former core code) and new **Samsung Tizen** (`samsungtv`: remote-control API over `wss` on port 8002 with token pairing, on/off, volume, HDMI inputs, standby detection), both in the official catalog.
- New capability `tv.control` and SDK 5 (`ITvProvider`, `ITvLink`, `ITvSession`, `RegisterTvProvider`). A TV plugin never opens a connection itself: everything goes through a connection of the core that reaches only the one address on the home network, only the declared ports, TLS with the certificate pinned at pairing, no proxy and no redirects, with limits on size, time and number of connections. Plain text only after explicit consent at pairing (expert mode); on plain ports the core refuses requests in which it recognises the key (also URL-encoded, as base64 or hex, as a JSON or XML character reference; a best-effort safety net against plugin bugs, not a barrier against malicious code). If TLS fails on a TLS port (handshake refused, connection reset), no plain port is allowed any more in that pairing. This PC's own address is never a TV. Only the plugin that paired gets the key, and only while it runs. Third-party plugins with `tv.control` may not hold their own network, native code or programs. The core checks and cleans every answer of a plugin; a hanging plugin holds nothing up.
- Pairings from 0.6 are kept: until the LG plugin is installed the TV page shows "Install the plugin for LG webOS" (opens the catalog entry) and CouchHUD suggests it once; afterwards the pairing works without confirming on the TV again. Without a TV plugin the page offers the catalog; the search shows the kind of every TV found and offers a missing plugin, typing an address asks for the kind when there are several.
- The names `webostv` and `samsungtv` are reserved for CouchHUD's own plugins, as are the kinds `lg-webos` and `samsung-tizen`. The core requires a plugin from CouchHUD's own catalog for these kinds; the folder name alone is not enough. Documented in `docs/PLUGINS.md` (controlling the TV, German).

**Second confirmation on the phone**
- Optional, off by default: critical actions (lock, codes, parental controls and profile rules, sign-in and shell, PC mode, phone rights and pairing, plugin rights, Offline) need a yes on a paired phone with the right *Full* after the code: the TV shows a number that is typed on the phone (no choices), 60 seconds, once, bound to exactly that action and the confirming phone; failed tries lock in steps (1 minute, 10 minutes, 1 hour), new questions at most every 10 seconds. Installing plugins from other sources, leaving a child's profile, quitting as the shell and the switch to light mode count too. It can only be switched on with the code check and such a phone; the warning for switching the code off gets stronger with it; emergency way through a Windows administrator. Limits in SECURITY.md.
- Fixes: switching a plugin back on needs the second confirmation when it is not CouchHUD's own or may use the network, the assistant or programs (switching off stays free). A companion rule that also starts on the locked couch or beside every game needs it too. Uninstalling a plugin no longer deletes the parents' function levels, so a reinstall cannot lift a function lock. The PC mode tray icon goes away at once when leaving (no race with entering again).


**Phone: encrypted connection, live sync and the library**
- Phones paired from now on talk to the PC over an encrypted connection, with no certificate and no app, on iPhone (Safari) and Android (Chrome). The QR code carries the address and a one-time secret in the part after `#`, which the browser never sends; PC and phone derive a key from it. Every request and every answer is encrypted, signed and numbered (no replay), the pairing itself too; neither a device token nor a code crosses the Wi-Fi in clear. The pairing code is no longer shown as text on the TV, only inside the QR code.
- Live: the PC tells the phone over an event channel, encrypted as well, when the library, collections, downloads, the on-screen keyboard, the sleep timer or the phone's rights change; the PC stays the only source and the phone fetches the new state. What the phone changes shows on the TV at once (collections, favourites, names, sleep timer).
- New tabs on the phone: *Control* (text, buttons, sleep timer, power), *Library* (search, All/Games/Apps/Favourites/collections, covers, start, favourite) and *Downloads* (progress). With full rights also rename, hide and show again (including the list of hidden entries) and create, rename, delete and fill collections. When a game is already running, the phone asks like the TV does.
- Sleep timer from the phone (30/60/90/120 minutes, shut down or standby, off): the TV runs it like one set on the TV (with the warning shortly before) and shows it on the phone. It follows the same rules as power from the phone (setting, couch lock, areas, desktop mode).
- Settings from the phone (with *Full* only, not in PC mode): a new *Settings* tab with a small fixed choice: skin, language, the Favourites tab (mode, period, websites), screensaver (delay, style), and sounds and volumes. Everything else (couch lock and codes, parental controls, profiles, shell and sign-in, services, privacy switches, plugins and their rights, catalog sources, mirroring Windows notifications, the phones' rights) cannot be changed from a phone; the PC checks every key and value against a fixed list with type and range. Values go through the same setters as on the TV; where the TV asks for the parents' code (screensaver with parental controls and several profiles), the phone is asked too, over the same encrypted parents' code path. Changes on the TV appear on the phone and the other way round (a new language reloads the TV page as soon as no code entry, dialog or profile picker is open).
- On the phone: *Reload artwork* in an entry's actions (with *Full*), like the edit menu on the TV. Internal: unused text `remote.codeInvalid` removed, two ASP0016 warnings in the phone server fixed.
- Rights per phone under *Settings › Phone › Paired phones*: *Control only* (default for new phones), *Library* or *Full*; changing them needs the parents' code. A phone of a child profile gets at most what the child may do on the TV, and never more than *Library*. Phones paired before 0.7 keep their current rights (text, buttons, power) over the old connection until they are paired again.
- Security (review of the phone remote): in PC mode (where neither the couch lock nor parental controls apply) every phone is only *Control only*, whatever level it has; library, start, rename, hide and collections are refused by the PC with a clear hint, the state reports the lowered level and the phone page explains it. A running sleep timer (set at the TV or from a phone) ends as soon as PC mode begins and can neither start nor run out there. Switching the sleep timer off from a phone needs the same clearance as setting it; a child's phone ends only a timer a child's phone set. Session, downloads and collections go to the phone as a fixed selection of fields, the name of a running game only from *Library* on and only when it would be visible in the phone's library. A request that fails the seal no longer renews the pairing and counts as a wrong try per address (after 5 the address rests for 30 seconds). SECURITY.md now states the limits against an active attacker on the Wi-Fi openly (intercepted parents' code, QR secret, device key, metadata, CSP) and recommends typing the parents' code at the TV.
- Security (second review of the phone remote): there is no "session" call for phones any more (it would have handed out the game's name unfiltered); the question before switching off names the game only when that phone's library would show it, otherwise it says "A game is running". Collections on the phone contain only entries of the library visible to that phone, a collection without a visible entry is left out. The brake on wrong tries per address is never emptied on overflow any more (hard cap, the oldest entry makes room), counts IPv6 by /64 and IPv4-mapped addresses as IPv4, sweeps at most once a second, never pauses loopback, and clears the count only after an admitted request, not already after a replayed one. A child's phone does not replace a running timer of a parent or the TV; the PC tells a phone's timer by a random token instead of a time window. Pairing uses the same brake (its own table): a hard cap instead of emptying at 256 entries, /64 for IPv6, loopback never paused, and a new QR code does not lift a pause.
- Everything goes through the same host calls and rules as on the TV (couch lock, profile, areas, parental grants, function levels). Where the TV asks for the parents' code, it may be typed on the phone only when the couch lock is the CouchHUD code and the phone is not a child's phone, only over the encrypted connection, only right after the PC asked for it for exactly this action, and at most 5 tries in 10 minutes; otherwise the phone says "confirm on the TV".
- For UI work: the preview server plays a paired PC over the encrypted channel too (`node scripts/ui-shot.mjs --mobile 1 --path "remote/index.html#k=AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA"`, level with `PHONE_LEVEL`).

**Companion apps: an app starts with the game**
- New under *Settings › Library › Companion apps* (part of the Games area): an app from the library (Wand, Discord, a monitoring tool …) starts with every game or with the games you pick. Per app: *Starts with*, *End with the game* (off by default), *Start minimized in the background* (on by default) and *Also start on a locked couch* (off by default). There is no input for paths or parameters: you only choose among the library's apps.
- **The game stays in front:** The app starts only once the game is in front (after the game start, waiting at most 15 seconds), without a right to the foreground and with a show command that does not activate. If a window takes the focus anyway, it is minimized at once (never ended). A program that asks for administrator rights does not start (no Windows prompt over the game). An app that runs already does not start again and is not ended later either.
- **Ending:** Only with the option on, only a process CouchHUD started itself, and only while it is the same program file with the same start time (a process id alone is not enough; the start time is checked on the handle that ends it). A polite close first, then ended after a grace period. Windows, CouchHUD, other sessions and the game's processes are left alone.
- **No way around the locks:** A companion app starts only what the person at the controller could start themselves. In a child's profile only apps on the child's list and without a parents' code needed; nothing with the area switched off or with a protected profile not yet chosen; nothing on a locked couch (unless the rule allows it and the program is not a tool, see below). Consoles, script files (`.bat`, `.cmd`), Windows tools, browsers, game clients, Store apps, protocols, web pages and programs from the network can never be companion apps. Checked when the rule is made and again at every start, on the start the library decides on at that moment. Rules are changed only unlocked and, with parental controls, with the parents' code; remote, MCP, assistant and plugins have no way to them. Details: SECURITY.md.
- **Improved after the security review:** The real path of the file is checked (links, junctions and 8.3 names resolved by Windows on the open handle; data streams, wildcards and `..` are never opened; a link to a program with another name is refused), when the rule is made, before every start and on the held file. The new process's start time and program file come from the process handle, and a process is ended only if the program file on the ending handle matches too. The focus guard minimizes only the companion app itself and its process tree, never foreign new windows; Windows' own programs, dialogs and other sessions are left alone. The locks are checked again before every single start. "Set up Wand" exists only for a validly signed `Wand.exe` (Wand or WeMod), otherwise at most "Add as an app" with a warning and the full path. The Wand hint comes only with the couch unlocked. The player detection counts only processes from the entry's program path, install folder or package folder.
- **Stricter Wand signature:** WinVerifyTrust checks the held file (open handle), the signer comes from its verification state, the chain is built by CouchHUD with only the machine roots and the Code Signing usage, and the publisher (O or CN) must be exactly on a small list. The rule keeps the SHA-256; if the file changes, the signature is checked again before the start, otherwise nothing starts ("Signature invalid").

**Apps in the Guide menu**
- Any app of the library can be put into the Guide menu from its details (*Edit › Put in the Guide menu*), as a quick start during a game (up to 12). The start goes through the library's own start with the same checks (couch lock, areas, child profile, parents' code); on a locked couch and for apps the child may not start, the menu does not show them.

**Wand is a companion app now**
- The Wand plugin is retired: Wand is a normal app and runs as a companion app. A household that has the plugin gets one hint; under *Companion apps* there is "Set up Wand" when `Wand.exe` is at one of its usual places (adds the app, puts it in the Guide menu, starts it with every game if you like). The plugin is never removed silently. The plugin example in the repository (`plugins/CouchHud.Plugin.Wand`) is only an SDK example without a catalog entry now, its emoji icons are gone, and it is no longer suggested as a plugin.

**Kodi, Emby, Jellyfin and Plex: compatibility only**
- The players start as a normal app from CouchHUD, and the Guide button leads back. Known program names (`kodi`, `EmbyTheater`, `JellyfinMediaPlayer`, `Plex HTPC` …) and the Store version of Kodi count in the running-detection: a launcher that hands over to the real player, or an app without an install folder, no longer ends the session too early. Kodi is added with `-fs` (full screen), Jellyfin with `--fullscreen`; changeable in the startup options. Nothing is shipped or downloaded, and a profile grants no rights.

**Catalog: categories**
- The catalog groups plugins and skins by category (Smart home, Streaming, Hardware, Media, AI, Information, HUD, Skins, Other). The *Category* row at the top of the list switches with A between *All* (with headings) and a single category, the cursor stays on it; updates come first in each group. Only categories that have entries show.
- The category is a field of each entry in the signed index (`category`). The names come from the program (German and English), never as free text from the index; an unknown or missing category falls back to *Other* (a skin to *Skins*). Older indexes without the field work unchanged.

**Uninstalled plugins whose files are still held: an honest state instead of "Error"**
- A plugin that does not confirm stopping when it is uninstalled (no `IDisposable`, or `Dispose` too slow) is cut off at once, but its files stay until the next start. Until then the plugin list showed it as "Error" and offered switching it on and off. It now says "Uninstalled – removed at the next start" with a note, the plugin's page shows only that note, and the catalog offers nothing for it. Once the folder is really gone, the entry leaves the list at once.
- Fail-closed: such a plugin can be neither switched on, approved nor loaded again until the restart (not even as "starts after restart"); the host refuses it, also with a stale list. The `plugin-trash` folder is never loaded and is emptied at the start as soon as its files are free (tests).
- Approving, accepting a withdrawal and recording such a folder are refused (the mark stays, or it would run again after the restart); a catalog install over it says "removed at the next start – install it again afterwards" instead of silently staging an update.

**Plugins without a restart**
- Installing (catalog and `.zip`), approving, switching on and allowing a withdrawn plugin again start the plugin at once. Switching off and uninstalling take it out of CouchHUD at once (tabs, pages, settings, quick actions, tools, functions, events); then it is asked to stop (`IDisposable`, at most 5 seconds). If it confirms, it is fully stopped and its files are free; otherwise the UI says that its own background work only ends after a restart, and switching it on again waits for the restart (never two copies side by side). Updates stay with the restart.
- Starting goes through the same code as at program start (approved list, revocation list, switch, Offline mode, capabilities, function levels). A plugin's files are opened and held before the hash is taken, so what is checked is what is loaded; the load context loads only checked files, native libraries too (own `DllImport` resolver; other developers' plugins may otherwise load only system libraries from System32). After switching off a plugin reads no settings any more and all its subscriptions to host events are gone. A plugin is invisible until `Initialize` has ended; when the start fails or hangs (30 seconds), everything is taken back. This also applies to the normal start: a hanging `Initialize` no longer holds up the other plugins.
- For plugin developers: if you start timers, tasks or connections, implement `IDisposable`. See [docs/PLUGINS.md](docs/PLUGINS.md) and [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md).

**Game switch, boot screen and the way to the app mode: follow-ups**
- Follow-up 5 to the game switch: bringing the new game forward gives up as soon as the person brings the HUD to the front (Guide button, profile switch, PC mode, unlock confirmation; automatics such as the foreground guard do not count); it also ends when the person goes to PC mode, the couch browser or the controller mouse. If the person came back to the HUD during the start and the new game's first window takes the front anyway (the start had granted it the right), the HUD takes it back once: only for the new game, only in the two seconds after its first window and only within that one minute. Known limit: a window the person activates themselves (Alt+Tab, a click) is not noticed; if they do it in exactly those two seconds, the HUD takes the front back, checks profile and parental rules first like "Back to the game", and changes nothing until a window shows (no forgotten unlock, no thawing of other games). One loop per switch. The undo reports a lost parents' code only if there was one before; if parental controls cannot be read it says "could not be checked, please look".
- The way to the app mode no longer starts while parental controls are on: the step "App mode: remove desktop start and lock" is refused by the service in that case anyway, until now only as the last step, after the others had run and were undone again. The message "Turn parental controls off first" now stands on the page, nothing runs, and an entry opens the parental controls settings. The service's own check is unchanged.
- A parents' code that was set at the open couch is lost when the lock is switched on (intended: a code planted there never becomes the lock). The setup now says so beforehand, and when "Cancel and undo everything" cannot bring it back, the undo hint names it: "The parents' code has to be set again". Nothing is skipped or weakened, it is only a message.
- Follow-up 4: After a game switch the new game stayed behind the HUD and keys did not reach it. The switch starts the game only after the question and the closing of the old game, long after the last key press, and Windows then no longer lets the foreground pass to the new game. CouchHUD now brings the new game forward itself once it shows a window (same checks and means as "Back to the game", parental rules included); it gives up after a minute and gives way if the person has gone back to the HUD with the Guide button meanwhile.

**Downloads: on the locked couch the page asks for the code instead of only saying “Unlock first”**
- In 0.6.3 “Search for updates” (X) and every update row (Windows, apps, CouchHUD, plugins and skins) only said “Unlock first” on the locked couch and never asked for the code, so no update could be reached. The same press now opens the code entry (the Windows sign-in in Windows mode), and the search or the update runs afterwards. Cancelled, nothing happens and nothing flashes busy.
- When the unlock runs out while a question is open and the host refuses the call for that reason, the page asks for the code once and tries again (search, cancelling a download, starting an update, the CouchHUD update). The host still checks the lock itself.

**Startup options: a batch file in the game's main folder works as start target**
- In 0.6.3 a `.cmd` in the "Call of Duty" folder was refused as own start target: for a found game the exe's folder counts as the game's folder, for Call of Duty that is `_retail_`. When the exe sits in such a program folder (`_retail_`, `bin`, `Binaries\Win64`, `x64` …), a `.bat`/`.cmd` may now also be in the main folder above, and so may its working folder. Never further up than the game's folder (no library folder such as `steamapps\common`, no drive, no program folders); an `.exe` stays in the exe's folder. The file's content stays pinned as before.
- A refusal now says how to do it: for a batch file it names the folder the file belongs in; for an exe outside it suggests the program in the game's folder or a `.bat`/`.cmd` in it.
- When the host wants the parents' code for the change, the page asks for it and saves once more, instead of only saying "The parent code is required" (happened when the page had not loaded the parental status yet).

**Couch lock: expert mode can no longer be switched while locked**
- In 0.6.3 expert mode was one of the settings a guest could change on the locked couch (like sound and look). It shows the admin's tools, though (startup options, start target, developer settings). The host now refuses the switch on the locked couch; the settings then ask for the code (or the Windows sign-in) and switch afterwards.

**Plugin settings: the lock first, then the keyboard**
- In 0.6.3 a locked plugin setting opened the on-screen keyboard (or the choice) first and then refused the typed value. Now the page asks the host first: on the locked couch an admin setting brings up the code entry, in a child's profile the parents' code, "nobody" the refusal right away, and only then the editor opens. The host still checks the change itself as before.

**Damaged code store: the way out at the code entry**
- With a damaged code store of the CouchHUD service the code entry in 0.6.3 only said "could not check the code", and the reset was offered only in Settings behind the lock. Now the code entry (couch and parents' code) offers the reset itself as soon as the service reports the file as damaged; Windows asks for an administrator. Afterwards the couch is open for two minutes to set the new code. When that time has passed, an administrator opens the lock without a code as long as the service knows none. A read error or an existing code offer none of this (see SECURITY.md).

**Security review 0.7.0: parents' releases after the reset, script folders, small fixes**
- After the reset of a damaged code store, with parental controls on, every parents' release counted as "an unlocked couch is enough" during the two minutes after it (and after every administrator unlock). Now the configuration remembers the reset (`ParentCodeResetPending`, set by the host only, ended by the new code), and every parents' release asks for the administrator's approval per action until then, as the security notes describe.
- A batch file as start target never reaches a library folder through program-folder names: walking up stops when the parent folder is or contains a watched game folder, contains another entry's install folder, is or holds `steamapps`/`steamapps\common`, or is `C:\Users`, `C:\ProgramData`, `C:\Users\Public` (`D:\Games\bin\game.exe` used to reach `D:\Games`). A linked main folder is checked too.
- After the reset the code entry shows "Code store reset" instead of the stale "damaged" message; the parents' prompt says that a second Windows question follows for the release. Downloads asks for the code only once when two calls are refused shortly after each other. Plugin settings use the same check as the settings pages.
- Re-check: clearing the hash and setting the marker after the reset happen in one single write (a crash in between reopened the hole). A kept couch code without a parents' code does not end the marker. A main folder that is a link is also refused when a start script is picked.

**Security: service-state reset and sleep timer notice tightened**
- When the "Reset service state" job in the service still runs after the wait, CouchHUD no longer sets the policies again at once (the job's end would remove them again): it asks the job in the background until it reports "done" or "failed" (three minutes at most), then sets this account's policies and paused services again and looks once more after ten seconds. The user is told honestly that it is still running, never "done". The set-again also runs when anything goes wrong on the way. The confirmation question, SECURITY and the guide now say that other accounts stay unlocked until their next CouchHUD start. Tests for the polling, the cap, the failure path and the second look.
- The sleep timer's notice over a game takes "timer started" only with at least 60 seconds left and at most once per 45 seconds (cancelling clears the gap); one start yields at most one notice, after that the timer is spent. Between two notices at least five minutes pass, whatever is cancelled and restarted in between, and a notice comes only from 90 seconds before the end; a replaced timer tells the host about the cancel too. The service-state follow-up keeps polling every 30 seconds after the three minutes (15 in all) and sets again only at the end, paused services are set on the UI thread, and the second look logs errors. Tests for that.

**Checks: the browser UI test in `check.sh` and CI**
- `scripts/browser-ui-check.mjs` (the couch browser's HUD side in a headless Edge, `--headless=new --mute-audio`, no window, no sound) now runs in `scripts/check.sh` and in CI. Without an Edge (variable `EDGE` or the usual install places on Windows, Linux and macOS) or without Node 22 it reports "skipped" with the reason and does not hold the check up.

**Mode switch: services as they were after an undo**
- A services selection that was only ticked, not applied, before the setup ended up applied (the services paused) after an undo. The switch now also records which services were really applied: the undo puts exactly those back and then only puts the ticks back, so a pending selection stays pending. This holds for the setup and for the way back; records from 0.6.3 are undone as before.
- Switching to the light mode now resumes the paused services also when the selection was already empty but the services were still applied.

**Steam settings: no Run entry without a reason**
- The `CouchHudReleaseSteamGuard` Run entry now follows the state on every pass: when nothing is left to put back (values back, state file gone or done by another process) it is removed, also while Steam runs. Before, it could stay in that case and start CouchHUD at every sign-in for nothing.
- Unchanged and intended: while Steam runs and couch values are still in Steam's files, the entry stays. Steam is never ended to put values back; they go back as soon as Steam is off, otherwise through the Run entry after the next sign-in.

**Mode › App: “CouchHUD stays in front” stays when you switched it on**
- The way back to the app mode now switches “CouchHUD stays in front” (suppress competitors) off only if the shell setup switched it on. If it was on before or you switched it on yourself, it stays on and the step shows “Not needed”. CouchHUD remembers for this whether the setup switched it on; any change of the setting of your own clears that mark. An undo puts the setting and the mark back.
- A shell setup from 0.6.3 has no mark: there the setting stays on on the way back and can be switched off in *Settings*.

**Sleep timer: the warning also over a game**
- The question a minute before the end (“Cancel” / “Now”) was only in the HUD and could not be seen behind a running game. Now a notice appears over the game and CouchHUD brings the HUD with the open question to the front, so it can be answered with the controller. In PC mode only the notice comes. With the HUD already in front nothing changes. The call works on a locked couch too (like the timer itself) and knows only the two timer actions; the text is the host's own. The notice comes only for the timer the page announced to the host when it started, only shortly before its end, and at most every 45 seconds.

**Reset service state in Settings**
- New entry under Windows integration (behind the couch lock, with a confirmation question): when the CouchHUD service's state is damaged, the service releases what it still knows, keeps the file aside and starts fresh. Before, that took a program run as administrator. The service now also takes an administrator's UAC approval for exactly this action (like the code store), never the couch or parents' code; a parents' code is not even checked for it. CouchHUD then sets this account's lock policies and paused services again as the settings say.

**Browser UI test strict in CI**
- CI sets up Node 22 itself; without Edge or Node 22 there the browser UI test fails instead of being skipped quietly. A watchdog ends it after 180 seconds without a result, and the CI step has its own time limit. Locally `SKIP_BROWSER_UI=1` leaves it out of `scripts/check.sh`.

**Downloads in sections**
- The list in *Downloads* is now split into sections with a small heading and a count instead of showing games, Windows and app updates mixed: *Running now*, then the waiting updates by kind (*CouchHUD update*, *App updates (winget)*, *Windows updates* with a restart that is due, *Plugins and skins*), finally *Finished*. Empty sections are left out; up/down still goes from row to row, the heading of a section's first row stays in view while scrolling, mouse clicks hit the right row. Update rows no longer name their source twice (the heading does now). Lock and parents' code stay as before.
- "Install games" is now on the View button in the *Games* tab (hint at the bottom right, V on the keyboard, mouse or touch on the hint; also with an empty or filtered list) instead of Y in *Downloads*: Downloads only shows what loads and what can be updated. The "Games not installed" entry in the Games tab's X menu stays. Without the Gaming area the button is not there.

**App updates: percent, confirmations and winget wherever it is**
- The percentage of app updates now really shows. winget draws its bars only into a real console, never into redirected output; so CouchHUD runs `winget upgrade` under a pseudo console (ConPTY, invisible, no window) and reads its screen through a small VT screen in Core (cursor jumps, rows only partly repainted, blanks as "cursor forward", erase and scroll, wide characters such as CJK and emoji taking two cells). The existing parser gets whole lines from it; winget's taskbar progress (OSC 9;4) also counts as a percentage. When the pseudo console is not available or does not start, winget runs redirected as before (counter, phases and sizes, no bars). The WinGet COM API would have been another dependency with its own activation and would not have used the proven parser.
- When an update needs a confirmation, Windows' prompt (UAC) or the installer's window used to sit behind CouchHUD's full screen; the prompt only blinked on a taskbar the couch does not have. Now winget may take the foreground when it starts (so the prompt comes right away), and while winget runs CouchHUD looks twice a second for the prompt of this session and for windows of programs winget started (checked by parent chain and start times). If there is one, the HUD steps aside and brings exactly that window forward, with a one-time note; on an open couch the controller becomes a mouse for an installer window. That only helps with installers that run without administrator rights: Windows keeps input of a normal program away from a window with higher rights, such an installer window needs mouse or keyboard (or comes forward by itself). While a game, the browser or PC mode runs, the HUD does not step aside, it only says once that a question is waiting; the same when another program's window is in front. If the installer keeps the same window, the HUD does not flip between two windows. Once nothing has been open for a moment (about 1.5 seconds), the HUD comes back, but only when nothing, the HUD itself or a window of the update is in front; if another program has taken the foreground meanwhile, it stays behind it (and never comes over a game, the browser or PC mode). The controller mouse the update started ends when the HUD comes back, when the update lets go of the screen, or when a game, PC mode or the browser starts; the bare desktop counts as an empty front, so the HUD comes back onto it. The prompt itself runs on the secure desktop, which no program and no controller can reach: it is answered with mouse or keyboard. Couch lock, foreground guard and parental approvals are unchanged.
- winget is no longer started by its bare name but by a full path: first the absolute, local entries of PATH, then `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WindowsApps\winget.exe` (often missing from PATH in fresh profiles). Only what exists is taken, and only from folders a standard account cannot write to (Windows, Program Files) or the real App Execution Alias in WindowsApps (a reparse point of that kind, not a file placed there). The check is on the start of the path, not on the access rights; code that already runs as the same user could swap the alias in the own WindowsApps folder between the check and the start - the same boundary as with PATH and the own files, with no gain in rights (winget elevates only through Windows' prompt). Never the program or working folder, no relative entries, no network paths; it starts in the system folder. If winget is missing, the message says so.
- A package id from winget's output is treated as untrusted: only letters, digits and `. - _ + \ { } ~ &`, at most 128 characters, never starting with "-". Other rows do not appear in the list, and only an id from the current list is upgraded. The command line for the pseudo console is quoted from the fixed argument list by Windows' rules.

**Back into the game works, CouchHUD no longer pushes itself in front**
- "Resume game" brought the game window forward with a plain call that Windows often refuses for controller input ("Couldn't bring the game window to the front"), and chose the biggest visible window by its current size: a minimized full-screen game is only icon-sized then, so a helper window won. Now CouchHUD takes the same robust way it uses for itself (briefly attaching input, raising the window, an Alt tap if need be), chooses the window by its normal size, skips invisible, cloaked, tool and tiny windows, prefers main windows to dialogs and thaws a frozen game first. If it fails, the reason is in the log.
- The game clients kept on standby in couch mode pulled CouchHUD to the front again 10 and 25 seconds after start, even while a game was starting or the browser was open. When CouchHUD may come forward on its own is now one rule for every automatic action: never over a running or starting game, the controller-as-mouse mode, PC mode, the browser or a capture. The Guide button always may.

**Switch games like on a console**
- Starting the running game again brings it forward instead of starting it a second time. Another game asks "<game> will be closed" with *Start new game* and *Cancel* (preselected). After the yes the running game is asked to close, ended after 10 seconds if need be, and only once it is gone the new one starts; if it still runs after 15 seconds, nothing starts. Everything the new start checks (lock, areas, parental controls, start target) is checked before anything is closed.
- Only the processes CouchHUD noted for the game (process id, start time and program file checked on the same handle, no mix-up with a reused id) and what they started are ended; game clients such as Steam (even one the game started itself), Windows processes and CouchHUD never. The yes only counts for exactly the game still running: if something changed meanwhile, CouchHUD asks again. With parental controls the child may switch too, since it only ends the running game (like "Quit game"); apps and websites still don't start beside a game. The assistant and MCP get a note that the switch has to be confirmed first instead of a question.
- Follow-up: when a game starts Steam (or another client), Steam and whatever Steam starts afterwards are no longer part of the game session. Before, the session then never ended: the switch failed with "still running", play time kept counting, and a later quit would have hit what Steam had started too. When the client itself is the library entry (Playnite in full screen), it is still ended when the time runs out. "Resume game" and starting the running game again no longer bring forward a game that parental controls are holding. While the switch waits for the old game to close, CouchHUD keeps checking: if the parents' one-time permission was used up meanwhile, the time ran out or another game runs, the old game is not ended by force and the switch is called off. A switch in progress counts for a plain start too.
- Follow-up 2: after the yes the switch could never start the new game (the start asked "is a switch already running?" about its own switch and refused, after the old game was already closed). The switch's final start now bypasses that check but still checks lock, areas, parental rules and start target like any start; the order (close the old one, wait for the tracker, start the new one) is tested. The check explicitly runs on the UI thread; if the check itself fails the switch counts as called off and nothing is ended by force. Right before the forced end the checks run once more (lock, parental rules, game changed). If the switch is called off after the old game is already closed, the message says so. The task list no longer brings forward a game that parental controls are holding.
- Follow-up 3: right before the new game starts, the switch checks once more whether another game (or an outside start) took over meanwhile; then no second game starts. If the start fails after the old game was closed, the message says the old game was closed and gives the reason. A plain start or MCP during a running switch now reports "switch in progress" instead of "close the game first".

**PC mode: a tap on the Xbox button takes you back to the couch**
- Until now PC mode was only left by holding the Guide button (or through the tray icon), and that was written nowhere. Now a tap is enough, as on the couch; whoever gave the tap to the Game Bar or set *Hold Guide only* under *Settings › Controller › Guide button in game* holds as before. In PC mode the button is handled before anything else, so a controller-as-mouse mode left running no longer catches it, and it is ended on the way back.
- Entering PC mode shows a short Windows notification "Xbox button: back to CouchHUD", and the question before the switch names the way back. While the desktop starts, the button waits; a click on the tray icon at that moment returns as soon as the desktop is up (before, the return could overtake the start of Explorer). If the same press opened the Game Bar as well, CouchHUD takes the front once more shortly after.
- Returning never weakens the lock: an unlock ends, policies, the foreground guard and the Game Bar/Steam guards come back as before. Entering PC mode still needs the unlock. Both are logged now (`[pcmode]`). *Restart into PC mode* stays for a permanent switch.
- Follow-up: when the Xbox button belongs to the Game Bar right now (CouchHUD left it there to be safe), the tap stays the Game Bar's in PC mode too and holding returns; the notification when entering says "hold" then. If entering PC mode fails early, the button no longer waits for ever.

**Game folders: the right program and a clean name**
- In watched game folders, CouchHUD now picks the program by points: closeness to the folder name, product name and size count; installers, uninstallers, crash reporters, checksum tools (RapidCRC, QuickSFV), anti-cheat, redistributables and subfolders such as `_CommonRedist` drop out. A repack used to show "RapidCRC Unicode" instead of "Mortal Kombat 1", an Unreal game "BootstrapPackagedGame"; for Unreal games the small launcher at the top of the folder now starts (it belongs to `…-Win64-Shipping.exe`), named after the folder. A Steam folder (or another game client) inside a game folder is no longer added as a game "Steam", nor anything under `steamapps`.
- The name is the file's product name when it fits the folder and is not just "Unity", "Unreal" or a publisher; signs such as ® and ™ drop out ("Call of Duty®" becomes the folder name "Call of Duty Modern Warfare 4" when that is more precise). Otherwise the cleaned folder name counts: versions, build numbers, "Repack", "MULTi12", "x64", "+ 3 DLCs", console ids and bracketed extras drop out, scene names ("Game.Name-GROUP") become words. When the picked file changed, the entry gets a new id: an earlier rename or picture choice for it no longer applies.

**Artwork: rather no picture than the wrong one, and "Search artwork"**
- Automatic matching only takes matches that surely fit the name: the same name after cleaning, the same without spaces, or a small typo with the same word count and numbers. Numbers, roman numerals and years must agree; a match that only starts the same no longer counts ("Mortal Kombat 1" used to get the pictures of "Mortal Kombat Arcade Kollection", SteamGridDB's first hit). Apostrophes don't count. Without a sure match the tile stays empty and the details page offers "Choose artwork".
- New in the edit menu: "Search artwork" lists the matches from the Steam store and SteamGridDB (with a key) for a search term you can change, with year, platform and thumbnail, the surest first. A pins the game: the pin (`artworkMatch` in the overrides, `steam:<AppID>` or `sgdb:<id>`) goes before the name per entry, survives every scan and "Reload artwork", has its own line in the artwork cache, and "Back to automatic" drops it. Online single-picture picks of the wrong game go with it; own pictures, rename and store match stay. Steam's own entries have no "Search artwork".
- Security: the host checks the pin itself (only `steam:` or `sgdb:` with a number, anything else is refused), clips the shown name and strips control characters, checks the search term like the store match does and reads the services' answers defensively. Thumbnails only come from the known artwork hosts. Searching and pinning are library edits: with the couch locked, unlock first, no parent code of its own. With online artwork off (privacy), the search asks no service and says so.

**Game folders and artwork: follow-ups**
- When the new selection picks another program than before, favourites, collections, "recent", play times, your changes (name, type, hidden, start options, store match, artwork pin) and above all the parents' lock lists in every profile move to the new id, once during the scan in a single step and without losing anything: a lock stays a lock (with state under both ids the locks are united and your own change wins), and the per-game time in the play time account follows the folder (each move is applied once per profile and in order; it is only recorded when the entry had saved settings). A second run changes nothing; games whose folder is gone are left alone.
- A folder only counts as a store client (Steam, Epic, GOG Galaxy, Ubisoft Connect, EA, Battle.net) when its main program is in it, not already for helpers such as `steamerrorreporter.exe` (a Source game keeps its entry). Folders under `steamapps` count too when the watched folder itself lies below it. "V2" in "Sniper Elite V2" is no longer removed as a version. At most 64 version resources are read per folder.
- Artwork from the name search that was remembered before the stricter matching (the wrong Mortal Kombat) is not reused; the first pass asks again. A typo hit only counts when it is the only one, and a difference of just a plural "s" ("Shadow Warrior" / "Shadow Warriors") is no typo. "Search artwork" loads thumbnails at most four at a time, a missing picture leaves the hit in the list, on a timeout the partial list comes with a note; search terms need a letter or digit, and the search needs a chosen profile like the pin does. Unpinning or repeating the same pin no longer removes the pictures you picked online, only pinning another game does.

**Library: hide entries, everywhere and for good**
- Y on an entry opens the same menu in Games, Apps, collections and on Home (favorite, collections, details, remove from collection) with the new item *Hide*. One short confirmation, then the entry leaves every list. Before, this was only hidden in details › Edit, without confirmation and not at all on a locked couch; Y on Home only toggled the favorite.
- Hidden stays hidden: CouchHUD keeps an ignore list in `config.json` (`Ignored`) with stable keys. Store games, Xbox apps and own entries are listed by their id; wrong guesses of the folder scan also by exe path and by game folder below the watched folder. When the scan picks another exe after an update (new id), the entry stays away. Hidden flags of earlier versions move onto the list once a scan finds the entry; until then they keep applying. Exe and game folder count only for finds of the core's own folder scan, never for plugin entries.
- To bring entries back: *Settings › Library › Hidden entries* ("Show again", with the controller). An own entry that is removed leaves the list too. Records of entries that are no longer in the library are listed by name only and can be removed. They are offered only after a complete scan: not before the first scan, not after a store, plugin or folder scan failed or was skipped, not while plugins are loading or paused (Offline mode), and not for entries of a watched folder or drive that is missing right now. Changing hidden and type in one edit uses up just one parents' code. The list holds at most 2000 entries (cut on load too); when it is full, CouchHUD says so instead of forgetting old ones. Entries that share a game folder change together right away.
- Hiding is a library change: the host demands an unlocked couch (otherwise the UI asks for the code) and, with parental controls, the parent code for hiding and showing again. The parents' code is used up only once every check passed; a refused edit (also through the assistant or MCP) never changes half. Hidden games stay visible to parental controls: play time, limits and locked entries still apply. Hiding only tidies up the lists; to block a game, use the game lock or the age rules.

**Tile pictures follow the skin, at once and both ways**
- Switching to a skin with square tiles (Deep Sea, Greenlight, Stardust) now shows the games' square pictures instead of squeezed or cropped covers, and switching back shows the covers again, without a restart and without a new scan. Until now the skin's choice was read only when a page was built, only on `:root`, and often before the skin's stylesheet had loaded.
- A skin names the picture a tile wants with `--tile-art: square | cover | hero` on the tile (or its row, or `:root`), where it sets the tile's shape. A missing picture gets a suitable stand-in (square → cover, cover → square, hero → cover → square). A picture that does not suit the tile's shape stands whole and centred over a blurred copy of itself instead of being stretched or cropped. The "Continue playing" card follows the same rule. Described in `docs/SKINS.en.md`.
- No new services are asked: square pictures still come only from the normal artwork lookup, under the switches in *Privacy*.
- Catalog skins: Deep Sea 1.0.3, Greenlight 1.0.3 and Stardust 1.0.6 ask for square pictures (older CouchHUD versions show them as before).

**Phone: standby, restart and shut down**
- Below the buttons the phone page has a *Power* section with standby, restart and shut down, for example to switch the PC off when the TV is already off. Every button asks on the phone first; "Cancel" is on top, the action below it in another place and only usable after a second. When a game runs on the TV, the question says so ("A game is running: …"). Afterwards the phone says what happens, or why not; the TV briefly shows which phone did what.
- It is the same way as the power menu on the TV (the same host call, no new operation, no call to the service) with the same rules: waiting profile picker, couch lock and switchable areas as in the bridge; whatever would need a code on the TV is refused on the phone instead of taking a code over Wi-Fi. Paired phones on the home network only, the same origin check as the other endpoints, a fixed list of three actions. Two steps with a one-time ticket (the same phone, the same action, 0.8 to 30 seconds, every try spends it), at most 10 requests a minute per phone and a 20-second pause after an action. The log names the action, the phone's short id and name, never the token or the ticket. The sleep timer stays on the TV.
- Never in desktop mode (PC mode): someone works at the PC then, and the phone says "The PC is in desktop mode – switch it off at the PC". New setting *Settings › Phone › Phone may shut down / restart the PC* (default on, behind the couch lock); off hides the section on the phone, and the PC refuses.
- Every request counts right after the pairing check, refused and unknown ones too; a throttled confirmation still spends its ticket. The 20-second pause has its own message and is lifted when the action did not run. The TV names the phone only once Windows took the command; when it fails the phone gets a fixed text and only the log gets the error message. The phone's name is cleaned the same way for the log and the TV (no control or format characters such as bidi overrides, at most 40 characters). After confirming, the question stays with "Working …" until the PC answered.
- For UI work: `scripts/ui-shot.mjs` knows `--path` (another page of `ui/`) and `--mobile 1`; the preview server plays a paired PC for the phone page that runs nothing.

**Development builds are pre-releases: the updater offers the release**
- Every build that is not a release build (local, CI without a tag) is called `0.7.0-dev+<commit>`; only the CI's tag build carries exactly `0.7.0`. A local test build with the release's number (0.6.3) had kept the updater from offering the release. The CI checks the version of the published `CouchHud.exe`; tests for the comparison (dev < release, release = release, never an update for itself).

**Checks: the browser UI test without port collisions, the skin check in a worktree too**
- `scripts/browser-ui-check.mjs` used fixed ports. Two runs at the same time (other worktrees, CI next to a local run) shared the Edge debug port and the preview server; one steered the other's browser, `import('/views/browser.js')` ran in a document that was not the app ("Failed to resolve module specifier"), and 22 FAILs followed. Now: free ports from the system, waiting for the preview server and the loaded app instead of a fixed pause, a clear message instead of follow-up failures.
- `scripts/skin-check.mjs` and `tabs-check.mjs` find the catalog checkout from a git worktree too (`COUCHHUD_CATALOG_SRC`, sibling folder, the main checkout through `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`). A run without skins says SKIPPED and fails with `RELEASE=1`.

**Power menu: three large tiles, the rest under "More ..."**
- The Start menu now shows only the everyday actions as three large tiles on top: sleep, shut down, restart; focus starts on sleep. The two groups "CouchHUD" and "Computer" with up to eight entries are gone.
- Below sits the collapsed "More ..." row (with `aria-expanded`). A or a click unfolds it in place with a short animation (none with reduced motion) and moves focus to its first entry: PC mode, restart into PC mode, restart CouchHUD, or "Quit CouchHUD" instead of PC mode in app mode. B folds the row back first and only then closes the menu. If nothing is left for the mode, the row is not there. Whether it is open only counts for the open menu.
- The sleep timer is one row showing its state ("Off" or "Shut down in 42 min."). X opens the timer picker from anywhere in the menu, and a new hint in the button bar says so. The picker itself (minutes, then action, B goes back) is unchanged.
- Cancelling a question brings the menu back on the same entry, unfolded for an entry under "More ...". Confirmations, the couch lock, parental rules and the red marking stay as they were; the mode still decides what is offered. Settings › Power shows the same entries in the new order, the Guide menu opens the new menu.

**Fixes and hardening**
- The Transparency page showed `HKLMSOFTWARECouchHud` for the automatic sign-in path instead of `HKLM\SOFTWARE\CouchHud`; the backslashes were missing.
- Exact-match checks (plugin and catalog ids, checksums, names in the image cache and in save backups, the setup checksum line) no longer let a trailing line break through.
- The service, the helper and the app load their system libraries from System32 only.
- Explorer and the Windows sign-in dialog shortcut (netplwiz) are started by their full path, not through the search path.
- The Downloads page no longer silently stops updating after a rare error while polling. Errors when returning from PC mode, when falling back to the desktop and when marking a healthy start now reach the log. The fall back to the desktop (when the HUD cannot start) now locks Windows off the interface thread and opens the desktop only once the lock works. If it does not, a window of its own says so, with Try again, Restart and Shut down (controller, keyboard, mouse), and it tries again by itself every few seconds.
- Tidied: unused code and unused texts (Home Assistant in the assistant) removed. In development, ESLint now checks the UI and the compiler a short list of real-bug rules.

**Security: the couch browser never opens a Windows dialog (audit M-1)**
- The script guard checks the whole event path (`composedPath()`) instead of only `event.target`, so file and color fields in a shadow DOM are recognised too. `attachShadow` is patched so closed shadow roots get the guard. Frames a script makes (`about:blank`, `srcdoc`) are patched on the first read of `contentWindow` and `contentDocument` and on load, and also run the script at `ContentLoading`. Dropping files from Windows is refused as well.
- The web view never shows a file chooser (DevTools protocol `Page.setInterceptFileChooserDialog`), handles the context menu itself and takes no files by drag and drop.
- New: `BrowserDialogGuard` closes, while the couch lock is on, not in PC mode and not with the developer switch, every standard dialog (open, save, print, color) in the couch browser's process tree at once (WinEvent hook plus a pass every 500 ms). Fail-closed: a WebView2 dialog whose owner cannot be told is closed, a dialog that does not close ends the couch browser, and without a running guard the browser does not open at all. The limits are in SECURITY.md.
- Follow-ups after review: the PDF viewer hides Print and Save, client certificate requests and screen shares are refused without a window. The guard now closes every visible top-level window of the couch browser that is not in the HUD's window tree or a known helper window (not only dialogs). With an unclear match only what belongs to a WebView2 process and whose owner chain leads to the HUD is closed; dialogs of other WebView2 programs stay. Without a known process id the couch browser does not run. On close, a surviving browser process (start time compared) is ended after about 1 s. Running the guard script more than once in the same window changes nothing any more.
- `rescan_library` needs the confirmation on the TV over MCP. The list of signed-in sessions is fail-closed (a failed query makes it "unknown"), and Windows Update never gets the couch or unlock grant.

**Security: pausing services and updates need more than any account (audit L-1, L-2)**
